<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> At any fixed positive computable viscosity, smooth forces can make a fixed fluid particle detect the halting of an arbitrary machine, starting from rest, while every mixed derivative of the force and velocity decreases faster than every inverse power of time. We give three complete memory constructions: compact moving curls, alternating fractional coordinates, and a periodic lattice on the flat three-torus. Each machine step takes one unit of physical time. The constructions retain earlier records at separated spatial scales and use an exact open detector with a shrinking signal.

- **claim-residual-realization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-realization)): Let $U$ be smooth and divergence free on $[0,\infty)\times\Omega$, with $U(0)=0$ and bounded $U,\nabla U$ on finite time intervals. On $\mathbb R^3$ suppose also that $U$ has the velocity regularity in <a href="#eq:energy-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-class">[eq:energy-class]</a>. Then $(U,0)$ is the unique solution with force $\mathcal R_\nu[U]$ in the classical class just specified. Its material flow is defined uniquely for all finite times. On $\mathbb T^3$, a mean-zero $U$ has mean-zero residual. The same uniqueness conclusion holds for an independently established reference solution $(U,P)$ in the class. In the noncompact uniqueness assertion, a bound on the competitor’s gradient may be omitted while keeping its bounded velocity and all other conditions in <a href="#eq:energy-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-class">[eq:energy-class]</a>. The reference field still has bounded gradient.
  - Explanation: A prescribed smooth incompressible velocity from rest is realized by its residual force. Uniqueness and a global material flow hold in the stated classical classes, including the specified noncompact competitor extension.
- **claim-effective-solenoidal-projection** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-projection)): Let $g$ be an effectively specified smooth mean-zero field on $[0,\infty)\times\mathbb T^3$, with effective bounds for every mixed derivative on finite time intervals. There is an effective smooth mean-zero $\phi$ with $\Delta\phi=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}g$. The field $g-\nabla\phi$ is solenoidal and mean zero. If $(U,P)$ solves <a href="#eq:NS" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:NS">[eq:NS]</a> for $g$, then $(U,P-\phi)$ solves it for $g-\nabla\phi$. Every bound of the form $\sup_{t,x}(1+t)^J\|\partial_t^l\partial_x^\alpha g\|<\infty$ for all $J,l,\alpha$ is preserved. Effective constants for the projected bounds can be computed from effective constants for the corresponding bounds on $g$ at finitely many higher spatial orders.
  - Explanation: Periodic projection effectively removes the gradient part of the force. It changes pressure while preserving velocity, spatial mean, and rapid mixed-derivative bounds.
- **claim-compact-curl-halting** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-rapid)): Fix a positive computable viscosity $\nu$. From a deterministic Turing machine $M$ and a finite input $w$ one can effectively construct a smooth force on $[0,\infty)\times\mathbb R^3$, supported for all times in the fixed compact set $$K=[-2,1]\times[-1,2]\times[-1,1],$$ such that, for every pair of nonnegative integers $J,l$ and spatial multi-index $\alpha$, $$\sup_{t\geq0,\,x\in\mathbb R^3} (1+t)^J\bigl\|\partial_t^l\partial_x^\alpha f(t,x)\bigr\|<\infty. \label{eq:rapid-force-bound}$$ The Navier–Stokes equation with this force and zero initial velocity has an explicit global smooth solution, unique in the finite-interval class <a href="#eq:energy-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-class">[eq:energy-class]</a>. Its material trajectory from the origin satisfies $$M\text{ halts on }w \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad X_1(t,0)<-1\text{ for some }t\geq0. \label{eq:rapid-event}$$ The velocity has the same fixed support and rapid mixed-derivative bounds. The pressure is zero. The force is a general body force. Because $K$ is fixed, the same bounds hold with $(1+t+\|x\|)^J$ in place of $(1+t)^J$.
  - Explanation: Moving compact curls implement local machine transitions while retaining earlier configuration records. Exact particle entry detects halting although the velocity, force, and every fixed mixed derivative decrease rapidly.
- **claim-torus-chart-halting** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-rapid-torus)): Fix a positive computable viscosity. From any deterministic Turing machine $M$ and finite input $w$, one can effectively construct on the unit flat torus $\mathbb T^3$ a smooth, solenoidal, spatially mean-zero force satisfying all the bounds <a href="#eq:rapid-force-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rapid-force-bound">[eq:rapid-force-bound]</a> with $\mathbb T^3$ in place of $\mathbb R^3$, with zero initial velocity, such that the unique smooth solution satisfies $$M\text{ halts on }w \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \frac14<X_1(t,a_*)<\frac38\text{ for some }t\geq0, \qquad a_*=(1/2,1/4,1/2).$$ Here the inequality denotes the indicated open coordinate slab of the torus. Pressure is normalized to mean zero, and uniqueness holds in the classical periodic class of Section <a href="#sec:analytic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:analytic">2</a>. The velocity and all its mixed derivatives also decrease faster than every inverse power of time.
  - Explanation: A compact velocity is embedded in an interior torus chart. Recomputing its residual and projecting the force preserves the exact particle event and rapid decay.
- **claim-alternating-memory-halting** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-alternating)): Fix a positive computable viscosity. From a deterministic Turing machine and a finite input one can effectively construct a smooth force $f$ on $[0,\infty)\times\mathbb R^3$, with spatial support contained in a fixed compact set independent of time, such that for every $J,l\geq0$ and spatial multi-index $\alpha$, $$\sup_{t\geq0,\,X\in\mathbb R^3} (1+t+\|X\|)^J \|\partial_t^l\partial_X^\alpha f(t,X)\|<\infty. \label{eq:alternating-schwartz}$$ The solution with zero initial velocity is explicit, global, and unique in the class <a href="#eq:energy-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-class">[eq:energy-class]</a>, with pressure zero. Its velocity satisfies the same support and derivative estimates. The material particle initially at $(-1,0,0)$ reaches $X_1>0$ if and only if the machine halts.
  - Explanation: The current instruction is decoded from a stationary donor coordinate while the next configuration is appended to the other memory. A separate shrinking signal detects halting without erasing records.
- **claim-periodic-lattice-halting** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-lattice)): Fix a computable viscosity $\nu>0$. A finite deterministic machine and a finite input effectively determine a smooth force $g$ on the unit flat torus $\mathbb T^3=(\mathbb R/\mathbb Z)^3$ with zero spatial mean. The Navier–Stokes equations with force $g$ and zero initial velocity have a smooth solution $(U,0)$ with zero spatial mean. Its particle from $$a_*=(1/4,1/2,1/2)$$ enters the fixed open set $$O=\{x\in\mathbb T^3:1/2<x_1<1\pmod 1\}$$ at some finite time if and only if the machine halts on that input. For every pair of nonnegative integers $r,J$ and every spatial multi-index $\alpha$, there is an effective constant $C_{r,\alpha,J}$ such that $$\label{eq:lattice-rapid} \\|\partial_t^r\partial_x^\alpha U(t)\\|_\infty +\\|\partial_t^r\partial_x^\alpha g(t)\\|_\infty \le C_{r,\alpha,J}(1+t)^{-J},\qquad t\ge0.$$ There is also an effective smooth mean-zero solenoidal force $f$ with the same bounds and exactly the same velocity and material event. It is $f=g-\nabla\phi$, with pressure $-\phi$, where $\phi$ is the mean-zero periodic solution of $\Delta\phi=\operatorname{div}g$.* *For either force, uniqueness holds among periodic classical solutions for which the velocity, its time derivative and its spatial derivatives through order two, and the pressure and its spatial gradient, are continuous on every $[0,T]\times\mathbb T^3$. Pressure is normalized to have zero spatial mean.
  - Explanation: Periodic residue arithmetic reads the newest configuration while retaining older records. A smooth planar motion is lifted to an incompressible torus velocity, and its residual or projected force realizes the same exact halting event.
- **claim-lattice-address-transition** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-lattice-address)): The functions $F_n,h_n$ on $\mathbb Z$ are $P_n$-periodic, $h_n\in\{0,1\}$, and $0\le F_n<P_{n+1}$ at every address. If $c_n$ is a true nonhalting configuration and $j\equiv c_n\pmod {P_n}$, then $F_n(j)=c_{n+1}$.
  - Explanation: The integer-address lookup is periodic and bounded at every address. On a genuine nonhalting configuration it returns the next complete configuration.
- **claim-lattice-scale-separation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-lattice-scales)): For $n\ge0$, $$\begin{aligned} P_n&\mid\epsilon_n^{-1},& P_n&\mid\epsilon_k/\epsilon_n\quad(0\le k<n), \label{eq:lattice-divisibility}\\ P_{n+1}\epsilon_{n+1} &\le\epsilon_n^{\,2^{2n+2}-1} \le\epsilon_n^3<\epsilon_n/4. \label{eq:lattice-plateau-bound} \end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $\beta_n$ decreases strictly and $\epsilon_n\le2^{-n}$.
  - Explanation: The scales make earlier records disappear from the current residue and keep the new displacement within the active selector plateau. Their growth also supplies rapid derivative decay.
- **claim-fixed-particle-undecidability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-undecidable)): For each of these four experiments, no algorithm decides the particle event for every force program produced by its construction. This remains true for the solenoidal mean-zero versions of the two torus experiments.
  - Explanation: A decision procedure for the constructed particle events would decide machine halting after composition with the effective compiler. The obstruction also holds for the projected torus forces.

Every bound of the form $\\sup_{t,x}(1+t)^J|\\partial_t^l\\partial_x^\\alpha g|<\\infty$ for all $J,l,\\alpha$ is preserved. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Flat torus** ([source](#eq:NS)): Write $\mathbb T=\mathbb R/\mathbb Z$.
- **Material flow** ([source](#eq:NS)): The material flow $X^u$ solves $\partial_tX^u(t,a)=u(t,X^u(t,a))$, $X^u(0,a)=a$.
- **Classical solution** ([source](#eq:energy-class)): A classical solution has $u,u_t$, spatial derivatives of $u$ through order two, $p$, and $\nabla p$ continuous on each finite closed time cylinder and satisfies <a href="#eq:NS" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:NS">[eq:NS]</a> pointwise.
- **Residual force** ([source](#sec:analytic)): For a smooth divergence-free field $U$ with $U(0)=0$, set $$\label{eq:residual} \mathcal R_\nu[U]=U_t+(U\cdot\nabla)U-\nu\Delta U.$$
- **Effective prescription** ([source](#pm-h-4)): An effective prescription takes the finite machine and word to a finite program that evaluates the force and every requested mixed derivative at computably supplied arguments to arbitrary prescribed rational error.
- **Addressed configuration code** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For the configuration $(q,\tau,i)$ at that time, put $$T=\sum_{v=-n}^{n+B}\operatorname{id}(\tau(v))A^{v+n},\qquad j=i+n,\qquad k_n=\operatorname{id}(q)+S(j+P_nT). \label{eq:rapid-code}$$
- **Addressed history coordinate** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Up to the first halt, store the actual configurations at $$y_n=\sum_{j=0}^n\delta_j k_j\in[0,1). \label{eq:rapid-history}$$
- **Moving localized curl** ([source](#eq:rapid-curl)): For a prescribed center path $c(\sigma)$ and a spatial scale $\delta>0$, the curl field $$\mathcal V(\sigma,x)= \nabla_x\times\left[ \zeta\left(\frac{x-c(\sigma)}{\delta}\right) \frac{\dot c(\sigma)\times(x-c(\sigma))}{2} \right] \label{eq:rapid-curl}$$ is divergence-free and equals $\dot c(\sigma)$ on a neighborhood of the moving center.
- **Fractional tape-stack codes** ([source](#eq:alternating-history)): After $n$ machine transitions let $\xi_n,\eta_n$ be the fractional base-$b$ codes of the tape strictly left of the head and from the head rightward, each nearest symbol first.
- **Alternating stationary donor** ([source](#pm-h-16)): On the plateau these velocities are respectively $(0,L_n,E_n)$ and $(L_n,0,E_n)$.
- **Least nonnegative residue** ([source](#pm-h-19)): For an arbitrary integer $j$, let $[j]_q$ denote its least nonnegative residue modulo the positive integer $q$.
- **Periodic lattice configuration code** ([source](#pm-h-19)): If the state number is $s$, the complete configuration therefore has the code $$\label{eq:lattice-code} c_n=L+b^{d_n}R+b^{2d_n}s,\qquad 0\le c_n<P_n.$$
- **Lattice selector bump** ([source](#eq:lattice-slot)): For $j\in\mathbb Z$, define a bump centered at the $j$th point of the scale-$n$ lattice by $$J_{n,j}(y)=\psi\bigl((y-y_*)/\epsilon_n-j\bigr).$$
- **Incompressible lattice lift** ([source](#pm-h-22)): Define on $\mathbb T^3$ $$\label{eq:lattice-lift} U(t,\xi,z)= \bigl(\eta'(z)v_1(t,\xi),\eta'(z)v_2(t,\xi), -\eta(z)\operatorname{div}_\xi v(t,\xi)\bigr).$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-compact-support-box | K | = | $[-2,1]\times[-1,2]\times[-1,1]$ | Fixed support for force and velocity, independent of execution and time. |
| q-addressed-grid-scale | \delta_n | = | $2^{-s_n}$ | The exponent depends on the machine and input length, independently of execution. |
| q-addressed-motion-power | b_n | ≤ | $\delta_n^{n+10}$ | Every transition index in the construction. |
| q-addressed-motion-separation | b_n | < | $\delta_n/8$ | The motion is smaller than the address spacing throughout each transition. |
| q-addressed-velocity-derivatives | \\|\partial_t^l\partial_x^\alpha W(t,\cdot)\\|_\infty | ≤ | $C_{l,\alpha}b_n\delta_n^{-\|\alpha\|}$ | Fixed mixed derivative orders; the constant is independent of the transition and grid address. |
| q-torus-chart-scale | \lambda | = | $1/8$ | Spatial velocity scaling before residual recomputation. |
| q-alternating-memory-scale | \epsilon_n | = | $b^{-s_n}$ | The scale exponent depends on input length and transition index. |
| q-alternating-slot-derivatives | \\|V_n\\|_{C^r} | ≤ | $C_r'\left(b^{-s_n} +b^{-s_{n+1}+(r+1)(s_n+1+K_n)}\right).$ | Fixed spatial order; constants depend on the table, base, and smooth profiles, independently of the slot. |
| q-lattice-period | P_n | = | $b^{p_n}$ | Stack width and state-field capacity at the transition index. |
| q-lattice-output-capacity | F_n | < | $P_{n+1}$ | Every integer address, regardless of whether the distinguished run visits it. |
| q-lattice-memory-scale | \epsilon_n | = | $b^{-K_n}$ | All scale indices in the lattice construction. |
| q-lattice-plateau-displacement | P_{n+1}\epsilon_{n+1} | < | $\epsilon_n/4$ | Every transition slot. |
| q-lattice-geometric-scale-majorant | \epsilon_n | ≤ | $2^{-n}$ | All lattice scale indices. |
| q-lattice-rapid-mixed-bounds | \\|\partial_t^r\partial_x^\alpha U(t)\\|_\infty+\\|\partial_t^r\partial_x^\alpha g(t)\\|_\infty | ≤ | $C_{r,\alpha,J}(1+t)^{-J}$ | All times and every fixed mixed derivative order and temporal weight. |

The elementary bounds are $$L_n\\leq C(n+1),\\qquad\n s_n\\geq16C(n+1),\\qquad\n \\frac{s_{n+1}}{s_n}=2^{2n+5}\\geq32(n+1).$$ These estimates give $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\sum_{n\\geq0}\\delta_n M_n\n       \\leq\\sum_{n\\geq0}2^{-15(n+1)}<1,\n       \\label{eq:rapid-total-memory}\\\\\n &b_n\\leq\\delta_n^{n+10}<\\delta_n/8,\n       \\label{eq:rapid-small-motion}\\\\\n &0\\leq j<n\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n       \\delta_j/\\delta_n\\text{ is an integer multiple of }M_n.\n       \\label{eq:rapid-divisibility}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For the second assertion, use $s_{n+1}-L_{n+1}\\geq(15/16)s_{n+1}\n\\geq30(n+1)s_n\\geq(n+10)s_n$. For the third, $s_n-L_n\\geq(15/16)s_n\\geq30s_{n-1}\\geq s_j$ when $j<n$, using $s_n/s_{n-1}\\geq32$. The strict inequality in <a href=\"#eq:rapid-small-motion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rapid-small-motion\">[eq:rapid-small-motion]</a> follows from $\\delta_n\\leq2^{-16}$.",

On this compact parameter set the expression has bounded $\\sigma,\\bar x$ derivatives of every order, uniformly bounded spatial support, and effective bounds. Disjointness of the summands therefore gives, independently of $n$ and $m$, $$\\|\\partial_t^l\\partial_x^\\alpha W(t,\\cdot)\\|_\\infty\n \\leq C_{l,\\alpha}b_n\\delta_n^{-|\\alpha|}\n \\qquad(1+n\\leq t\\leq2+n).\n \\label{eq:rapid-velocity-estimate}$$ In particular there is no factor counting the grid cells. <a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-realization-class** [claim](#stmt-lem-realization) / [proof](#stmt-lem-realization): Use the restated residual-realization and uniqueness lemma in its precise classical classes. -> Residual realization, uniqueness, and a material flow for all finite times.
- **step-periodic-projection** [claim](#stmt-lem-projection) / [proof](#eq:projection): Use the mean-zero periodic Poisson solution and the restated effective projection lemma. -> Solenoidal force with unchanged velocity and adjusted pressure.
- **step-addressed-local-table** [claim](#stmt-thm-rapid) / [proof](#eq:rapid-transition-range): Encode the complete finite configuration and define the transition lookup on every padded integer address. -> A bounded address lookup that equals the next code on actual nonterminal configurations.
- **step-addressed-history-read** [claim](#stmt-thm-rapid) / [proof](#eq:rapid-read): Separate dyadic scales so older configuration records contribute multiples of the current code capacity. -> The newest code is recoverable by a residue without deleting earlier records.
- **step-addressed-moving-curls** [claim](#stmt-thm-rapid) / [proof](#eq:rapid-field): Attach a moving localized curl to each grid address and use smaller-than-spacing displacements and zero time collars. -> A globally smooth divergence-free velocity with fixed compact support.
- **step-addressed-decay-realization** [claim](#stmt-thm-rapid) / [proof](#pm-h-9): Estimate each scaled curl uniformly, use disjointness to avoid counting cells, and differentiate the residual. -> Rapid mixed-derivative bounds, effective constants, and the unique global forced solution.
- **step-addressed-event-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-thm-rapid) / [proof](#pm-h-10): Identify the observed trajectory with its moving address center by induction and verify finite evaluation through enclosing intervals. -> Exact halting equivalence and a force program that does not precompute the input execution.
- **step-chart-transfer** [claim](#stmt-cor-rapid-torus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-rapid-torus): Scale the velocity into an interior torus chart, recompute its residual at the chosen viscosity, and project the force. -> Mean-zero solenoidal rapid forcing with the corresponding open slab event.
- **step-alternating-decoder** [claim](#stmt-thm-alternating) / [proof](#pm-h-15): Decode the state and fractional tape stacks with a smooth periodic fractional-part surrogate, then apply digit push and pop updates. -> Smooth donor functions giving the next configuration and the halting signal on intended donors.
- **step-alternating-stationary-write** [claim](#stmt-thm-alternating) / [proof](#pm-h-16): Alternate compact curl fields so the donor stays stationary while the receiver and signal move. Verify that the trajectory remains on the cutoff plateau. -> Retained alternating history and an exact halting signal with no intermediate false positive.
- **step-alternating-decay-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-thm-alternating) / [proof](#pm-h-17): Bound donor derivatives by the reading scale, absorb their cost into the smaller writing scale, and evaluate smooth profiles without discontinuous equality tests. -> Rapid force and velocity bounds, effective evaluation, and uniqueness in the stated Euclidean class.
- **step-lattice-address-lemma** [claim](#stmt-lem-lattice-address) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lattice-address): Extract residue fields and check the stack update bounds for each head-motion branch. -> Periodic address functions with bounded output and correct genuine transitions.
- **step-lattice-scale-lemma** [claim](#stmt-lem-lattice-scales) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lattice-scales): Compare scale exponents with code widths to establish divisibility and smaller-than-plateau displacement. -> Older records vanish from the current residue and the next write stays inside the selector plateau.
- **step-lattice-planar-event** [claim](#stmt-thm-lattice) / [proof](#pm-h-21): Use disjoint periodic selectors and smooth pulses. Prove the complete particle path stays on its active selector plateau and crosses the detector only on halting. -> A periodic planar motion with retained configuration history and exact open-set halting equivalence.
- **step-lattice-incompressible-realization** [claim](#stmt-thm-lattice) / [proof](#pm-h-22): Cancel planar divergence with the auxiliary periodic component, preserve the coding plane, estimate mixed derivatives, and form the residual force. -> Mean-zero incompressible velocity and residual force with the same event and effective rapid bounds.
- **step-lattice-effective-projection** [claim](#stmt-thm-lattice) / [proof](#pm-h-23): Evaluate finitely many enclosing lattice candidates and project the mean-zero residual, preserving pressure balance and the unique velocity. -> Effective residual and solenoidal force programs realizing exactly the same particle event.
- **step-reachability-undecidability** [claim](#stmt-cor-undecidable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-undecidable): Compose a hypothetical particle-event decider with each effective compiler and reduce symbol printing to halting. -> No total decision algorithm for the produced force programs in any of the fixed experiments.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-lattice-address-transition**: The lattice lookup is periodic in its address, stays within the next configuration capacity, and performs the prescribed transition on a genuine nonhalting configuration.
- **inv-retained-history-selector**: Earlier records disappear from the current residue without being erased, and the new record remains within the plateau of the selector that read its instruction.
- **inv-stationary-alternating-donor**: The alternating construction keeps the donor stationary throughout writing, so its decoded instruction remains valid while the receiver retains its earlier records.
- **inv-exact-halting-event**: The prescribed particle enters its fixed open detector exactly when the supplied machine halts; nonhalting trajectories remain outside even between coding times.
- **inv-rapid-mixed-decay**: Every fixed mixed derivative of the constructed velocity and force decreases faster than every inverse power of time, while machine transitions retain unit physical duration.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-precision-halting-oracle**: Treat a numerical particle detector with fixed measurement tolerance as a halting decision procedure. -> Late halting signals can be arbitrarily small, while nonhalting trajectories approach or occupy the detector boundary.
- **anti-bounded-tests-prove-reachability**: Use finite transition-table tests to certify the unrestricted fluid halting equivalence or decide eventual particle entry. -> The arithmetic checks cover finite address tables; the reachability theorem concerns a complete trajectory and potentially unbounded execution.
- **anti-real-equality-branching**: Choose an evaluation branch by deciding whether an arbitrary computable coordinate equals a cell boundary. -> Exact-coordinate encoding does not supply a general equality decision procedure.
- **anti-performance-guarantee**: Advertise the effective force compiler as an efficient simulator or a real-time implementation. -> Arbitrary rational-error evaluability and effective derivative bounds do not imply an efficiency estimate.
- **anti-viscosity-invariant-rescaling**: Rescale a Euclidean force directly into a torus chart while assuming that viscosity is unchanged by spatial scaling. -> The torus construction rescales the velocity and then recomputes its residual for the specified viscosity.
- **anti-arbitrary-force-global-existence**: Apply the explicit global solution construction to arbitrary external forces or initial velocities. -> The realization argument begins with a prescribed smooth divergence-free reference velocity and realizes its residual.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite constructive component is the integer residue lookup of the lattice address lemma. Each supplied finite alphabet, stack width, and normalized transition table determines a finite residue table, which can be checked exhaustively. The arithmetic checks do not certify the smooth fluid realization, the scale estimates for all slots, or unrestricted particle reachability. Source basis: The functions $F_n,h_n$ on $\mathbb Z$ are $P_n$-periodic, $h_n\in\{0,1\}$, and $0\le F_n<P_{n+1}$ at every address.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with b, d, states, alphabet, halting, table, lo, hi.
# PRECONDITIONS: b >= 2; d >= 1; states and alphabet in [1,b]; complete normalized nonhalting table; lo <= hi.
# OUTPUTS: JSON rows [address, transition_code, halting_indicator] for every address in the inclusive range.
# TIME: ABSENT from the source; finite exact enumeration only.
# SPACE: ABSENT from the source; output rows are retained.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact Python integers, residues, and powers.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and produces no accepted output.
import json
import sys

p = json.load(sys.stdin)
b = p['b']
d = p['d']
states = p['states']
alphabet = p['alphabet']
halting = set(p['halting'])
lo, hi = p['lo'], p['hi']
assert all(type(v) is int for v in [b, d, states, alphabet, lo, hi])
assert b >= 2 and d >= 1 and 1 <= states <= b and 1 <= alphabet <= b and lo <= hi
assert all(type(s) is int and 0 <= s < states for s in p['halting'])
table = {}
for row in p['table']:
    assert len(row) == 5 and all(type(v) is int for v in row)
    s, a, target, written, move = row
    assert 0 <= s < states and s not in halting and 0 <= a < alphabet
    assert 0 <= target < states and 0 <= written < alphabet and move in (-1, 0, 1)
    assert (s, a) not in table
    table[s, a] = (target, written, move)
assert set(table) == {(s, a) for s in range(states) if s not in halting for a in range(alphabet)}
width = b ** d
period = b ** (2 * d + 1)
next_width = b ** (d + 2)
next_period = b ** (2 * (d + 2) + 1)
rows = []
for j in range(lo, hi + 1):
    c = j % period
    L = c % width
    R = (c // width) % width
    s = c // (width * width)
    l = L % b
    a = R % b
    h = int(s in halting)
    F = 0
    if s < states and s not in halting and a < alphabet:
        target, written, move = table[s, a]
        if move == 1:
            left = written + b * L
            right = (R - a) // b
        elif move == -1:
            left = (L - l) // b
            right = l + b * written + b * (R - a)
        else:
            left = L
            right = R - a + written
        F = left + next_width * right + next_width * next_width * target
    assert 0 <= F < next_period and h in (0, 1)
    rows.append([j, F, h])
print(json.dumps(rows, separators=(',', ':')))
```

    "oracle_code": "import json\nimport sys\n\nwith open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8') as f:\n    p = json.load(f)\nwith open(sys.argv[2], encoding='utf-8') as f:\n    got = json.load(f)\nif sys.argv[3] != 'address-table-valid':\n    raise SystemExit(1)\nb, d = p['b'], p['d']\nstates, alphabet = p['states'], p['alphabet']\nlo, hi = p['lo'], p['hi']\nassert all(type(v) is int for v in [b, d, states, alphabet, lo, hi])\nassert b >= 2 and d >= 1 and 1 <= states <= b and 1 <= alphabet <= b and lo <= hi\nhalt = p['halting']\nassert all(type(s) is int and 0 <= s < states for s in halt)\nrules = p['table']\nassert all(isinstance(r, list) and len(r) == 5 and all(type(v) is int for v in r) for r in rules)\nfor s in range(states):\n    for a in range(alphabet):\n        matches = [r for r in rules if r[0] == s and r[1] == a]\n        assert len(matches) == (0 if s in halt else 1)\nfor s, a, target, written, move in rules:\n    assert 0 <= s < states and s not in halt and 0 <= a < alphabet\n    assert 0 <= target < states and 0 <= written < alphabet and move in (-1, 0, 1)\nassert isinstance(got, list) and len(got) == hi - lo + 1\n\ndef digits_of_address(j):\n    digits = []\n    for unused in range(2 * d + 1):\n        j, digit = divmod(j, b)\n        digits.append(digit)\n    return digits\n\ndef encode(digits):\n    value = 0\n    for digit in reversed(digits):\n        value = value * b + digit\n    return value\n\ncapacity = b ** (2 * (d + 2) + 1)\nfor offset, j in enumerate(range(lo, hi + 1)):\n    row = got[offset]\n    assert isinstance(row, list) and len(row) == 3 and all(type(v) is int for v in row)\n    assert row[0] == j\n    digits = digits_of_address(j)\n    left = digits[:d]\n    right = digits[d:2*d]\n    state = digits[-1]\n    expected_h = 1 if state in halt else 0\n    expected_f = 0\n    if state < states and state not in halt and right[0] < alphabet:\n        matching = [r for r in rules if r[0] == state and r[1] == right[0]]\n        assert len(matching) == 1\n        unused_s, unused_a, target, written, move = matching[0]\n        if move == 1:\n            new_left = [written] + left\n            new_right = right[1:]\n        elif move == -1:\n            new_left = left[1:]\n            new_right = [left[0], written] + right[1:]\n        else:\n            new_left = left[:]\n            new_right = [written] + right[1:]\n        new_left += [0] * (d + 2 - len(new_left))\n        new_right += [0] * (d + 2 - len(new_right))\n        expected_f = encode(new_left + new_right + [target])\n    assert 0 <= row[1] < capacity\n    assert row[2] in (0, 1)\n    assert row[1] == expected_f and row[2] == expected_h\n",

Source basis: The functions $F_n,h_n$ on $\\mathbb Z$ are $P_n$-periodic, $h_n\\in\\{0,1\\}$, and $0\\le F_n<P_{n+1}$ at every address.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON with b, d, states, alphabet, halting, table, lo, hi.\n# PRECONDITIONS: b >= 2; d >= 1; states and alphabet in [1,b]; complete normalized nonhalting table; lo <= hi.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON rows [address, transition_code, halting_indicator] for every address in the inclusive range.\n# TIME: ABSENT from the source; finite exact enumeration only.\n# SPACE: ABSENT from the source; output rows are retained.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact Python integers, residues, and powers.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and produces no accepted output.\nimport json\nimport sys\n\np = json.load(sys.stdin)\nb = p['b']\nd = p['d']\nstates = p['states']\nalphabet = p['alphabet']\nhalting = set(p['halting'])\nlo, hi = p['lo'], p['hi']\nassert all(type(v) is int for v in [b, d, states, alphabet, lo, hi])\nassert b >= 2 and d >= 1 and 1 <= states <= b and 1 <= alphabet <= b and lo <= hi\nassert all(type(s) is int and 0 <= s < states for s in p['halting'])\ntable = {}\nfor row in p['table']:\n    assert len(row) == 5 and all(type(v) is int for v in row)\n    s, a, target, written, move = row\n    assert 0 <= s < states and s not in halting and 0 <= a < alphabet\n    assert 0 <= target < states and 0 <= written < alphabet and move in (-1, 0, 1)\n    assert (s, a) not in table\n    table[s, a] = (target, written, move)\nassert set(table) == {(s, a) for s in range(states) if s not in halting for a in range(alphabet)}\nwidth = b ** d\nperiod = b ** (2 * d + 1)\nnext_width = b ** (d + 2)\nnext_period = b ** (2 * (d + 2) + 1)\nrows = []\nfor j in range(lo, hi + 1):\n    c = j % period\n    L = c % width\n    R = (c // width) % width\n    s = c // (width * width)\n    l = L % b\n    a = R % b\n    h = int(s in halting)\n    F = 0\n    if s < states and s not in halting and a < alphabet:\n        target, written, move = table[s, a]\n        if move == 1:\n            left = written + b * L\n            right = (R - a) // b\n        elif move == -1:\n            left = (L - l) // b\n            right = l + b * written + b * (R - a)\n        else:\n            left = L\n            right = R - a + written\n        F = left + next_width * right + next_width * next_width * target\n    assert 0 <= F < next_period and h in (0, 1)\n    rows.append([j, F, h])\nprint(json.dumps(rows, separators=(',', ':')))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The effective prescription constructs a finite program that evaluates the force and requested mixed derivatives to prescribed rational error. This supports a field evaluator with certified enclosures, while the eventual particle event remains undecidable. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The distinguished trajectories depend on exact coordinates and shrinking halting signals. The construction does not establish a detector tolerance suitable for a simulation using fixed measurement precision. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The periodic lattice construction supplies a concrete local evaluation procedure: enclose the coordinate, include every potentially active bump, and evaluate its coefficient by integer arithmetic and a transition lookup. This can support an enclosure-based field inspection tool. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The lattice transition formulas define an exact local algorithm on arbitrary integer addresses. Residue extraction and stack updates compute the next code for a valid nonhalting configuration without deciding the eventual outcome of its execution. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The addressed construction gives a concrete complete-configuration representation containing the state, absolute head position, and finite tape window. The slot index and integer code recover the configuration, including its blank extensions. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Effective evaluation is asserted without an efficiency estimate. The construction supplies no hardware execution contract or accelerator resource bound, and its increasingly fine spatial scales do not establish a fixed hardware precision requirement. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Particle-event undecidability is a reachability obstruction for a particular force-program family. It supplies no cryptographic primitive, adversary model, or robustness guarantee for a security mechanism. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The field coefficients implement a supplied deterministic instruction table. This is a prescribed computation construction rather than a learning procedure, and it offers no training or generalisation contract. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The prescribed field realizes a machine execution and an eventual reachability event. The paper does not construct an optimisation objective or a method for selecting an optimal trajectory; its undecidability result also blocks a universal exact reachability decision procedure for the constructed family. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Retained records reside in coordinates of a distinguished material particle. Their preservation resolves invertibility of a smooth flow; it does not provide replicated storage, interprocess communication, or a consistency protocol. |
| other (Mathematical fluid dynamics and computability) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The paper gives explicit smooth divergence-free velocities from rest and prescribes their residual forces. Its compact-curl, alternating-memory, and periodic-lattice constructions make exact particle reachability equivalent to machine halting while retaining rapid mixed-derivative decay. |

It is $f=g-\\nabla\\phi$, with pressure $-\\phi$, where $\\phi$ is the mean-zero periodic solution of $\\Delta\\phi=\\operatorname{div}g$.*\n\n*For either force, uniqueness holds among periodic classical solutions for which the velocity, its time derivative and its spatial derivatives through order two, and the pressure and its spatial gradient, are continuous on every $[0,T]\\times\\mathbb T^3$. <a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-computable-positive-viscosity** (hypothesis): Absolute effective evaluation assumes a positive computable viscosity. For a fixed noncomputable viscosity, the residual formulas have effectiveness only relative to that parameter. Blocked use: ['Claim an unconditional effective force evaluator for an arbitrary noncomputable viscosity.', 'Extend the construction to nonpositive viscosity.']
- **limit-prescribed-reference-solution** (formal_scope): Global smooth solutions are obtained by prescribing a smooth divergence-free reference velocity from rest and forming its residual. The result does not establish global smooth existence for arbitrary forces or initial velocities. Blocked use: ['Apply the construction as a global existence theorem for arbitrary prescribed forcing.', 'Apply residual realization without establishing the reference velocity and its initial value.']
- **limit-classical-uniqueness-regularity** (hypothesis): Uniqueness is restricted to the specified finite-interval classical classes. The Euclidean assertion retains the stated velocity and pressure regularity even when the competitor gradient bound is omitted. Blocked use: ['Infer uniqueness in an unspecified weak solution class.', 'Drop the Euclidean pressure integrability or bounded competitor velocity when using the competitor extension.']
- **limit-complete-configuration-addresses** (hypothesis): The addressed decoder needs the specified state numbering, head field, tape width, and slot index. Its range bound applies to padded addresses, but machine-transition correspondence applies to actual nonterminal configurations. Blocked use: ['Interpret every padded address as a genuine machine configuration.', 'Decode a configuration using the tape width or head convention from a different slot.']
- **limit-retained-history-scale-separation** (hypothesis): Reading the newest addressed record requires the prescribed scale divisibility, while realizing the transition by disjoint moving curls requires displacement smaller than the address separation. Earlier records remain stored. Blocked use: ['Erase older records while invoking the retained-history argument for a noninjective instruction.', 'Increase a transition displacement without re-establishing disjointness of moving supports and correctness of residue decoding.']
- **limit-stationary-donor-and-cutoff-plateau** (hypothesis): Alternating-memory correspondence requires the donor to remain stationary during its slot and the proposed trajectory to remain on the fixed cutoff plateau. The smooth periodic decoder agrees with fractional-part decoding only on the designated fractional range. Blocked use: ['Move the donor during writing while assuming its decoded instruction stays unchanged.', 'Use the plateau velocity formulas outside the plateau.', 'Treat the smooth periodic decoder as the fractional-part function on arbitrary real arguments.']
- **limit-periodic-lattice-address-correspondence** (hypothesis): The lattice rule is defined and bounded at every integer address. Its identification with a machine successor additionally requires a true nonhalting configuration and the current configuration residue. Unscanned padded digits need not be valid alphabet symbols. Blocked use: ['Claim that every bounded lattice output represents a valid machine execution.', 'Reject padded addresses solely because an unscanned digit does not represent an alphabet symbol.', 'Apply the successor correspondence to a halting configuration.']
- **limit-lattice-selector-plateau** (hypothesis): The lattice particle proof requires old-record divisibility and a new-record displacement that remains inside the active selector plateau. These conditions also make decoding independent of the chosen torus lift. Blocked use: ['Replace the scales with arbitrary decreasing scales without checking divisibility and plateau containment.', 'Allow the particle to leave the active selector plateau while retaining the displayed constant-instruction path.']
- **limit-incompressible-lift-coding-plane** (hypothesis): The planar lattice motion is not asserted to preserve area. The incompressible lift uses the specified auxiliary periodic profile and preserves the verified planar event on its invariant coding plane. Blocked use: ['Use the planar field directly as an incompressible fluid velocity without its lift.', 'Transfer the verified detector event to arbitrary initial positions outside the coding plane.']
- **limit-periodic-projection-and-pressure** (formal_scope): Effective solenoidal projection assumes an effective smooth mean-zero periodic field with effective derivative bounds. It changes pressure while preserving the reference velocity; it supplies no Euclidean compact-support preservation result. Blocked use: ['Apply the periodic projection lemma to an arbitrary nonperiodic force.', 'Keep the old pressure after subtracting the force gradient.', 'Assume solenoidal projection preserves Euclidean compact support.']
- **limit-residual-recomputation-after-scaling** (hypothesis): The torus chart transfer rescales the velocity and recomputes its residual at the specified viscosity. The proof does not assume viscosity invariance under spatial scaling. Blocked use: ['Rescale the Euclidean force directly while keeping the same viscosity without recomputing the residual.']
- **limit-fixed-order-rapid-bounds** (formal_scope): Rapid decay is asserted for every fixed mixed derivative order and temporal weight, with constants depending on the requested orders. The estimates absorb derivative costs only after an order-dependent initial range. Blocked use: ['Treat the bounds as a single uniform estimate over all derivative orders.', 'Discard the finite initial slots when establishing an effective bound for a requested order.']
- **limit-effective-evaluation-without-equality-tests** (formal_scope): Effective field evaluation uses rational enclosures, finitely many candidate slots or spatial translates, and flat cutoff bounds. Exact coordinate storage does not provide a general equality decision procedure for computable reals. Blocked use: ['Require exact equality testing to choose a time slot or lattice cell.', 'Discard a potentially active spatial term solely from a rounded coordinate.']
- **limit-effectiveness-without-efficiency** (unknown): The force and derivative evaluators are effective, but the paper asserts no efficiency estimate or implementation resource bound. Blocked use: ['Claim real-time force evaluation.', 'Assign a time or space complexity to the complete compiler without separate analysis.', 'Infer a fixed arithmetic precision sufficient for all execution times.']
- **limit-exact-observation-and-shrinking-signals** (approximation): The detector equivalences concern exact trajectories and open events. Halting signals tend to zero, while nonhalting trajectories approach or occupy detector boundaries. No uniform positive separation or fixed perturbation tolerance is established. Blocked use: ['Treat failure to detect a rounded trajectory as a proof of nonhalting.', 'Transfer the equivalence to a detector with fixed measurement tolerance.', 'Claim robustness under a fixed field perturbation.']
- **limit-unrestricted-reachability-and-finite-checks** (formal_scope): Undecidability concerns the force programs produced by the fixed experiments. A finite check of integer transition arithmetic does not decide eventual particle entry or certify the full analytic construction over unrestricted execution. Blocked use: ['Promote finite transition-table agreement to a decision procedure for eventual reachability.', 'Claim that a bounded arithmetic test verifies all scale estimates, smoothness, uniqueness, or the full trajectory.', 'Extend the corollary to an unspecified force-program family without a reduction.']
- **limit-unit-time-steps-and-no-onto-slowdown** (formal_scope): The constructions shrink spatial displacement while retaining unit-duration physical steps. Their conclusion is not obtained by an onto clock slowdown with a rapidly decreasing nonnegative clock derivative. Blocked use: ['Explain the construction by an integrable clock derivative that nevertheless traverses infinitely many internal steps.', 'Interpret increasing spatial precision as infinitely many instructions accumulating at finite physical time.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 376 (`lean/docs/376.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Geometric Programs for Solenoidal Forcing](../../preprints/Geometric-Programs-for-Solenoidal-Forcing-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Computation under Rapidly Vanishing Navier–Stokes Forcing](../../preprints/Computation-under-Rapidly-Vanishing-Navier-Stokes-Forcing-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Velocity-Field Detection of Computation in Forced Navier–Stokes Flows](../../preprints/Velocity-Field-Detection-of-Computation-in-Forced-Navier-Stokes-Flows-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Incompressible Box Transport and Finite Computation](../../preprints/Incompressible-Box-Transport-and-Finite-Computation-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization realizes finite families of prescribed positive diagonal maps on coding sheets by smooth incompressible flows on a flat three-torus. At every positive computable viscosity, it supplies a solution starting from rest with zero pressure and a force that is divergence free, has spatial mean zero, and is one-periodic from time zero. Each map acts on every point of its source sheet, and the construction provides effective bounds for all mixed derivatives.
>
> This selected statement covers the sheet programs. The paper's fixed-particle halting detector and its reciprocal maps on solid boxes are outside its scope.
>
> The formalization proves that a fluid particle can detect arbitrary finite-machine halting using alternating-coordinate memory in smooth incompressible Navier–Stokes flows whose velocity and force decay faster than every inverse power of time, including every mixed derivative. For each fixed positive computable viscosity, the construction starts from rest and provides effective forces, unique material trajectories, and an exact equivalence between entering a fixed open detector and halting.
>
> The velocity and force share one compact support for every machine and input. The solution-uniqueness assertion uses the stated energy comparison class.
>
> The formalization constructs smooth forced three-dimensional Navier–Stokes flows from rest whose velocity fields detect whether a prescribed machine halts, at any positive computable viscosity. On the unit flat torus, halting is equivalent to the vertical velocity exceeding $1/2$ somewhere in a fixed observation strip. On $\mathbb R^2\times\mathbb T$, it is equivalent to the integral of the nonnegative vertical velocity over the observation half-plane times the circle exceeding $1/2$.
>
> The torus force is confined to a fixed horizontal region. The cylindrical force has globally bounded mixed derivatives and compact horizontal support on every finite time interval. Both constructions have effective descriptions and uniqueness within their respective smooth solution-comparison classes.
>
> The formalization realizes finitely many positive diagonal affine maps of determinant one between rational solid boxes by a smooth compactly supported divergence-free flow. Source boxes are pairwise disjoint and target boxes are pairwise disjoint, while overlap between the two families is allowed. Each map holds on a neighborhood of the entire source box, and the velocity is supported in the middle half of the time interval.
>
> It also encodes every finite machine and input in a smooth, compactly supported incompressible Navier–Stokes flow on $\mathbb R^3$ starting from rest: the particle initially at $(4,0,0)$ enters the fixed open box $(-1,2)^3$ exactly when the machine halts. One velocity works for every viscosity $\nu>0$, with zero pressure and force $f_0+\nu f_1$. Velocity and force have bounded mixed derivatives and are one-periodic after time one; the repeated velocity depends only on the machine. The force is computable for computable $\nu$. Velocity is unique among smooth zero-data solutions with $u\in C_tH_x^2\cap C_t^1L_x^2$, bounded $u$ and $\nabla u$ on finite time intervals, and pressure modulo a function of time in $C_tH_x^1$.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open scoped BigOperators ENNReal Topology ContDiff
open MeasureTheory

namespace AlternatingNS

abbrev Rule := ℕ × ℕ × Fin 3

abbrev Machine := ℕ × ℕ × ℕ × List ℕ × List (Option Rule)

namespace Machine

def states (M : Machine) : ℕ := M.1 + 1

def alphabet (M : Machine) : ℕ := M.2.1 + 1

def initialState (M : Machine) : ℕ := M.2.2.1

def haltingStates (M : Machine) : List ℕ := M.2.2.2.1

def table (M : Machine) : List (Option Rule) := M.2.2.2.2

def instruction (M : Machine) (q a : ℕ) : Option Rule :=
  (M.table[q * M.alphabet + a]?).join

def WellFormed (M : Machine) : Prop :=
  M.initialState < M.states ∧
  (∀ q ∈ M.haltingStates, q < M.states) ∧
  ∀ q a r, q < M.states → a < M.alphabet → M.instruction q a = some r →
    r.1 < M.states ∧ r.2.1 < M.alphabet

def ValidInput (M : Machine) (w : List ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ a ∈ w, a < M.alphabet

structure Configuration where
  state : ℕ
  head : ℤ
  tape : ℤ → ℕ

def isHalting (M : Machine) (c : Configuration) : Bool :=
  decide (c.state = M.states ∨ c.state ∈ M.haltingStates)

def initial (M : Machine) (w : List ℕ) : Configuration where
  state := M.initialState
  head := 0
  tape := fun i => if 0 ≤ i then w[i.toNat]?.getD 0 else 0

def step (M : Machine) (c : Configuration) : Configuration :=
  if M.isHalting c then c else
    match M.instruction c.state (c.tape c.head) with
    | none => { c with state := M.states }
    | some r =>
      { state := r.1
        head := c.head + ((r.2.2.val : ℤ) - 1)
        tape := Function.update c.tape c.head r.2.1 }

def run (M : Machine) (w : List ℕ) (n : ℕ) : Configuration :=
  (M.step)^[n] (M.initial w)

def Halts (M : Machine) (w : List ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ n : ℕ, M.isHalting (M.run w n) = true

end Machine

abbrev Space := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin 3)
abbrev Field (E : Type*) := ℝ → Space → E
abbrev Velocity := Field Space
abbrev Pressure := Field ℝ
abbrev MultiIndex := Fin 3 → ℕ

noncomputable section

def e (i : Fin 3) : Space := EuclideanSpace.single i 1

def dx {E : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E]
    (i : Fin 3) (F : Field E) : Field E :=
  fun t x => fderiv ℝ (F t) x (e i)

def dt {E : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E]
    (F : Field E) : Field E :=
  fun t x => fderivWithin ℝ (fun s => F s x) (Set.Ici 0) t 1

def spatial {E : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E]
    (α : MultiIndex) (F : Field E) : Field E :=
  (dx 0)^[α 0] ((dx 1)^[α 1] ((dx 2)^[α 2] F))

def mixed {E : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E]
    (l : ℕ) (α : MultiIndex) (F : Field E) : Field E :=
  dt^[l] (spatial α F)

def div (u : Velocity) (t : ℝ) (x : Space) : ℝ :=
  ∑ i : Fin 3, dx i u t x i

def grad (p : Pressure) (t : ℝ) (x : Space) : Space :=
  (EuclideanSpace.equiv (Fin 3) ℝ).symm (fun i => dx i p t x)

def laplacian (u : Velocity) (t : ℝ) (x : Space) : Space :=
  ∑ i : Fin 3, dx i (dx i u) t x

def advection (u : Velocity) (t : ℝ) (x : Space) : Space :=
  fderiv ℝ (u t) x (u t x)

def residual (ν : ℝ) (u : Velocity) : Velocity :=
  fun t x => dt u t x + advection u t x - ν • laplacian u t x

def timeCylinder (T : ℝ) : Set (ℝ × Space) := Set.Icc 0 T ×ˢ Set.univ

def Smooth {E : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E]
    (F : Field E) : Prop :=
  ContDiffOn ℝ (∞) (Function.uncurry F) (Set.Ici 0 ×ˢ Set.univ)

def ClassicalRegularity (u : Velocity) (p : Pressure) : Prop :=
  (∀ x, DifferentiableOn ℝ (fun t => u t x) (Set.Ici 0)) ∧
  (∀ t, 0 ≤ t → Differentiable ℝ (u t)) ∧
  (∀ t, 0 ≤ t → ∀ i, Differentiable ℝ (dx i u t)) ∧
  (∀ t, 0 ≤ t → Differentiable ℝ (p t)) ∧
  ∀ T, 0 ≤ T →
    ContinuousOn (Function.uncurry u) (timeCylinder T) ∧
    ContinuousOn (Function.uncurry (dt u)) (timeCylinder T) ∧
    (∀ i, ContinuousOn (Function.uncurry (dx i u)) (timeCylinder T)) ∧
    (∀ i j, ContinuousOn (Function.uncurry (dx i (dx j u))) (timeCylinder T)) ∧
    ContinuousOn (Function.uncurry p) (timeCylinder T) ∧
    (∀ i, ContinuousOn (Function.uncurry (dx i p)) (timeCylinder T))

def ContinuousInH {E : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E]
    (k : ℕ) (F : Field E) (T : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ α : MultiIndex, (∑ i, α i) ≤ k →
    ∃ g : ℝ → Lp E 2 (volume : Measure Space),
      ContinuousOn g (Set.Icc 0 T) ∧
      ∀ t ∈ Set.Icc 0 T,
        ∀ᵐ x ∂(volume : Measure Space), (g t) x = spatial α F t x

def C1InL2 (u : Velocity) (T : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∃ g g' : ℝ → Lp Space 2 (volume : Measure Space),
    ContinuousOn g (Set.Icc 0 T) ∧
    ContinuousOn g' (Set.Icc 0 T) ∧
    (∀ t ∈ Set.Icc 0 T, HasDerivWithinAt g (g' t) (Set.Icc 0 T) t) ∧
    (∀ t ∈ Set.Icc 0 T, ∀ᵐ x ∂(volume : Measure Space), (g t) x = u t x) ∧
    (∀ t ∈ Set.Icc 0 T, ∀ᵐ x ∂(volume : Measure Space), (g' t) x = dt u t x)

def CompetitorEnergyClass (u : Velocity) (p : Pressure) : Prop :=
  ∀ T, 0 ≤ T →
    ContinuousInH 2 u T ∧ C1InL2 u T ∧ ContinuousInH 1 p T ∧
    ∃ C : ℝ, ∀ t ∈ Set.Icc 0 T, ∀ x, ‖u t x‖ ≤ C

def EnergyClass (u : Velocity) (p : Pressure) : Prop :=
  CompetitorEnergyClass u p ∧
  ∀ T, 0 ≤ T → ∃ C : ℝ, ∀ t ∈ Set.Icc 0 T, ∀ x,
    ‖u t x‖ + ‖fderiv ℝ (u t) x‖ ≤ C

def NavierStokes (ν : ℝ) (f u : Velocity) (p : Pressure) : Prop :=
  ClassicalRegularity u p ∧
  (∀ x, u 0 x = 0) ∧
  ∀ t, 0 ≤ t → ∀ x,
    div u t x = 0 ∧
    dt u t x + advection u t x = -grad p t x + ν • laplacian u t x + f t x

def Rapid (F : Velocity) : Prop :=
  ∀ J l : ℕ, ∀ α : MultiIndex, ∃ C : ℝ, 0 ≤ C ∧
    ∀ t, 0 ≤ t → ∀ x,
      (1 + t + ‖x‖) ^ J * ‖mixed l α F t x‖ ≤ C

def SupportedIn (F : Velocity) (K : Set Space) : Prop :=
  ∀ t, 0 ≤ t → Function.support (F t) ⊆ K

def IsTrajectory (u : Velocity) (a : Space) (γ : ℝ → Space) : Prop :=
  γ 0 = a ∧ ∀ t, 0 ≤ t → HasDerivWithinAt γ (u t (γ t)) (Set.Ici 0) t

def observedParticle : Space := (EuclideanSpace.equiv (Fin 3) ℝ).symm ![-1, 0, 0]

abbrev RationalPoint := ℚ × ℚ × ℚ × ℚ
abbrev RationalVector := ℚ × ℚ × ℚ
abbrev IndexCode := ℕ × ℕ × ℕ
abbrev Program := Nat.Partrec.Code
abbrev FieldProgram := Program × Program × Program

def Returns {A B : Type*} [Encodable A] [Encodable B]
    (c : Program) (a : A) (b : B) : Prop :=
  Encodable.encode b ∈ c.eval (Encodable.encode a)

def index (α : IndexCode) : MultiIndex := ![α.1, α.2.1, α.2.2]

def rationalVector (q : RationalVector) : Space :=
  (EuclideanSpace.equiv (Fin 3) ℝ).symm ![(q.1 : ℝ), (q.2.1 : ℝ), (q.2.2 : ℝ)]

def rationalPoint (q : RationalPoint) : ℝ × Space :=
  ((q.1 : ℝ), rationalVector q.2)

def dyadicError (n : ℕ) : ℝ := (1 / 2 : ℝ) ^ n

def ComputableReal (r : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∃ a : ℕ → ℚ, Computable (fun n => ((a n).num, (a n).den)) ∧
    ∀ n, |r - (a n : ℝ)| ≤ dyadicError n

def ProgramFor (c : FieldProgram) (F : Velocity) : Prop :=
  (∀ (l : ℕ) (α : IndexCode) (q : RationalPoint) (k : ℕ),
    ∃ v : RationalVector, Returns c.1 (l, α, q, k) v ∧
      (0 ≤ q.1 →
        ‖rationalVector v - mixed l (index α) F (rationalPoint q).1 (rationalPoint q).2‖
          ≤ dyadicError k)) ∧
  (∀ (l : ℕ) (α : IndexCode) (R k : ℕ),
    ∃ m : ℕ, Returns c.2.1 (l, α, R, k) m ∧
      ∀ z z' : ℝ × Space,
        0 ≤ z.1 → 0 ≤ z'.1 → ‖z‖ ≤ R → ‖z'‖ ≤ R →
        ‖z - z'‖ ≤ dyadicError m →
        ‖mixed l (index α) F z.1 z.2 - mixed l (index α) F z'.1 z'.2‖ ≤ dyadicError k) ∧
  (∀ (J l : ℕ) (α : IndexCode),
    ∃ B : ℕ, Returns c.2.2 (J, l, α) B ∧
      ∀ t, 0 ≤ t → ∀ x,
        (1 + t + ‖x‖) ^ J * ‖mixed l (index α) F t x‖ ≤ B)

def AlternatingConclusion (ν : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∃ (compileForce compileVelocity : Machine × List ℕ → FieldProgram),
    Computable compileForce ∧ Computable compileVelocity ∧
    ∃ K : Set Space, IsCompact K ∧
      ∀ (M : Machine) (w : List ℕ), M.WellFormed → M.ValidInput w →
        ∃ (f U : Velocity) (X : Space → ℝ → Space),
          ProgramFor (compileForce (M, w)) f ∧
          ProgramFor (compileVelocity (M, w)) U ∧
          Smooth f ∧ Smooth U ∧
          SupportedIn f K ∧ SupportedIn U K ∧ Rapid f ∧ Rapid U ∧
          f = residual ν U ∧
          NavierStokes ν f U (fun _ _ => 0) ∧ EnergyClass U (fun _ _ => 0) ∧
          (∀ (v : Velocity) (p : Pressure),
            NavierStokes ν f v p → CompetitorEnergyClass v p →
            ∀ t, 0 ≤ t → ∀ x, v t x = U t x ∧ p t x = 0) ∧
          (∀ a, IsTrajectory U a (X a)) ∧
          (∀ a γ, IsTrajectory U a γ → ∀ t, 0 ≤ t → γ t = X a t) ∧
          ((∃ t : ℝ, 0 ≤ t ∧ 0 < X observedParticle t 0) ↔ M.Halts w)

end
end AlternatingNS

namespace AlternatingNS

theorem alternating (ν : ℝ) (hν : 0 < ν) (hc : ComputableReal ν) :
    AlternatingConclusion ν := by
  sorry

end AlternatingNS

end OAI
```
